We can also use combinatorial search over binary sequences with local search, which might yield good F. Let's examine binary sequences.

For nonnegative 0/1, the convolution (with itself) is the "additive energy profile". Sequences with small max sum multiplicity are "Barker sequences"? Barker has low autocorrelation (difference sums), not sum. But if we reverse half, difference becomes sum? If we take a sequence that is symmetric? Partition positions such that sum pairs correspond to differences? Suppose define binary code on group Z_n: condition i+j=k. If set j' = -j, then i - j' = k. So if sequence is invariant under j -> -j? For a binary sequence x, convolution x*x has same profile as autocorrelation x*rev(x). If x is palindromic? For a set A, sum mult i+j=k equals difference mult i - j = k if we map j to -j. If we choose A subset of cyclic group and use differences, low difference sets (difference sets/Barker) control max. Optimization phase for a given length n:
- Start with initial p (uniform, parent normalized, random with structure).
- Each iteration:
   - Compute q and score. If improved best.

Update via mirror:
```
def mirror_update(p, D, eta):
    floor = 1e-15 / n maybe
    p_safe = np.maximum(p, floor)
    u = np.log(p_safe) - eta * D
    # subtract max for stability
    u -= u.max()
    expu = np.exp(u)
    return expu / expu.sum()
```
What eta? D range. We can compute scale = D.max() - D.min(); if scale>0: base_eta = n / (scale * n?) Let's think. If p uniform, D_i ~? w sum1, D_i ~2/n times window fraction? Range maybe ~2/n. For n=6733, scale ~1e-4. To get log weight ratio e^{η range} ~e, need η~1/scale~n~6733. So mirror step eta should be ~n or larger. If eta=6733, update can be strong. Let's set base = max(1, n/(scale+eps)). Then try factors [0.1,0.3,1,3,10,30] * base? But if eta too large, p may become very spiky. We can line search.

Alternatively, Euclidean projection step: Δp ~ η D. D range ~1e-4, to move O(1/n)=1e-4, η~1. But moving only 1e-4 may slow; but many iterations. However, mirror with η~n gives O(1) changes.

Use both.

But objective nonconvex; large steps may jump to bad. Line search selects best by internal score. We can evaluate multiple candidates per iteration. For speed, mirror update with different eta only O(n) each; then q for candidate. Could be many.

We can also use high-low transfer:
```
def transfer_update(p, D, fraction, frac_indices=0.1):
   order = np.argsort(D)
   m = max(1, int(n*frac_indices))
   low_idx = order[:m]; high_idx = order[-m:]
   # move mass from high to low proportional to p high
   mass = fraction * p[high_idx].sum()
   if mass <=0: return p
   newp = p.copy()
   # for each high, reduce by fraction of its p
   reduction = fraction * newp[high_idx]
   newp[high_idx] -= reduction
   # distribute reduction to low proportional to current p low? or uniform low mass? To increase low, maybe proportional to low's existing + target.
   low_weights = newp[low_idx] + 1e-12
   newp[low_idx] += mass * low_weights / low_weights.sum()
   return clip normalize
```
But simply fraction may be too systematic. We can combine: high to low proportional to D difference.

Could also use "top activation" D: Instead of D from weighted gradient, choose top d lags exactly and compute D. Softmax maybe good.

Optimization method details:

Let's devise a robust `optimize_sequence(p, n, time_limit, rng, best_holder)`.

Internal functions:
- `normalize_nonnegative(p)`: p = np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s<=0 return uniform; p/=s; clip to 1000? We keep sum1 so max≤1.
- `score_and_q(p)`: returns score, q, maxq. Use FFT. Need ensure if p has tiny negative, clip.

   For step type, generate candidate and compute q/score; keep best if score < current.

```
def proj_simplex(z):
    # z length n
    u = z - z.max()
```

```
def proj_simplex(z):

    if not pos.any(): return np.full(n,1/n)
```

Transfer update:
We can make it more effective by using D to create target. Let D sorted. Compute `D_center = (D.max()+D.min())/2`. Let deficit_high = max(0, D - D_center), surplus_low = max(0, D_center - D). We want transfer mass from high to low proportional to p*deficit? New p high = p*(1 - alpha*deficit/ max_deficit) maybe; add distribution. But a simpler sort method:
```
def transfer_update(p, D, alpha):
    n=p.size
    order=np.argsort(D)
    m=max(1,int(n*0.1))
    low=order[:m]; high=order[-m:]
    # move up to alpha of high mass
    mass = alpha * p[high].sum()
    if mass <= 1e-15: return p
    newp=p.copy()
    # reduce high proportionally, not below 0
    newp[high] *= (1-alpha)
    # distribute added mass to low proportionally to (1 - D_low/median?) or just p_low + small
    low_weights = newp[low] + 1e-12
    newp[low] += mass * low_weights / low_weights.sum()
    return normalize(newp)
```
But if high contains very small p and low large, mass small. Could transfer more by selecting top D indices regardless p. Maybe use index fraction 0.3.

Alternative "rank update": Let ranks r = argsort(D) descending. Set newp_i ∝ p_i * exp(-alpha * (rank/n -0.5))? This is mirror but deterministic. Could just use mirror line search.

Maybe Euclidean projection with high step is enough.

Softness/focus schedule:
- Start with focus small (e.g., 5) to move away from uniform (all lags). Then increase focus (10,50,200) to polish peaks.
- For each focus, run iterations. After improvement, continue.
- Include random noise: p *= exp(σ random) normalized, σ=0.05-0.5; accept if better or with simulated annealing? Since objective scale invariant, random perturbations can escape.

We can use a simple simulated annealing/walk: Maintain current and best. Each iteration, propose candidate from current using optimizer; if worse, maybe accept with probability exp((-score-best/T))? But objective not normalized; use relative. Or just restart from best with random perturbation after stagnation.

Better: Multi-start local search:
- Generate a pool of initial sequences (uniform, parent, sinusoidal, random). For each, run local optimizer for a time slice. Keep best.
- Also periodically perturb best and reoptimize (restarts).

Since we can't know time to convergence, allocate time per start.

Random perturbation:
```
def random_perturb(p, rng, strength):
    p2 = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0, strength, size=n))
    return normalize(p2)
```

Loop implementation for active lags with weights list `(k,wk)`:
```
D = np.zeros(n)
for k,wk in active:
    s = max(0, k-n+1)
    e = min(n-1, k)
    length = e-s+1
    # p indices k-i for i=s..e: start pidx = k-s, end = k-e
    D[s:e+1] += wk * p[start_pix?][::-1]
```
We need efficient slice. Let `pidx_start = k - s`, `pidx_end = k - e`. The sequence p[pidx_start], p[pidx_start-1], ..., p[pidx_end]. Since pidx_start >= pidx_end. We can do:
```
seg = p[pidx_end:pidx_start+1][::-1]
D[s:s+len] += wk * seg
```
This creates segment copy. For active up to 1000, O(active*length) memory/time. Maybe okay if active small. Use FFT for many.

But we can compute D for all active lags using FFT if active count > threshold. We'll implement dense FFT always maybe simpler; N=32768, 2 extra FFTs. Let's assume okay.

Let's design local optimizer more concretely:
```
def run_local(p, deadline, rng, best_holder):
    n=p.size
    cache N = next_pow2(4*n)
    current = p.copy()
    current_score, q, qmax = score_q(current, N)
    best_local_score = current_score
    best_local = current.copy()
    stagnant=0
    focus_cycle = [5, 20, 80, 250]
    fi=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Official update if local best improved and time
        if current_score < best_holder.global_internal - 1e-10:
            # maybe official
        if qmax == 0: break
        focus = focus_cycle[fi % len]
        # occasionally vary focus based on stagnant
        if stagnant > 50:
            focus = focus_cycle[(fi+1)%len]
        w = make_weights(q, qmax, focus)
        D = grad_dense(w, current, N)
        if not finite D or all close: 
             D = np.zeros(n)
        # Generate candidates:
        candidates=[]
        dmean = np.mean(np.abs(D)) + 1e-30
        # mirror
        for mult in [0.5,1.5,5.0]:
            eta = mult / dmean
            candidates.append(mirror_update(current,D,eta))
        # Euclidean projection
        for mult in [0.5,2.0,10.0]:
```

```
def param_gradients(c, n, rng, focus=50):
   phi shape (D,n)
   logits = phi @ c
   p = softmax(logits)
   q, qmax = score_q_fixed(p)
   w = softmax(focus*(q-qmax)/qmax)
   D = grad_dense(w,p)  # derivative wrt p
   # derivative wrt c: φ_j dot (p*(D - mean(p*D)? Actually p=softmax(logits). ∂p_i/∂c_j = p_i(φ_{j,i} - mean(p φ_j)). L=∑_i D_i p_i. So grad_j =∑_i D_i p_i(φ_j - mean(pφ_j)) = dot(pD, φ_j) - (∑pD)*(∑p φ_j).
   pd = p*D
   s = pd.sum()
   grad = (phi @ pd) - s*(phi @ p)
   return p, score, grad
```

Implementation of `score_q`:
```
def score_q(p, N=None):
    n = p.size
    if N is None: N = next_pow2(4*n)
    Pf = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(Pf * Pf, N)
    L=2*n-1
    q = q[:L]
    # because p real nonnegative, q should real; clip small negative
    qmax = float(np.max(q))
    if qmax < 0: qmax=0
    # For internal score, use max(q) but if qmax tiny? sum p maybe not 1 due to normalization; normalize p before.
    s=p.sum()
    score = 2.0*n*qmax/(s*s)
    return score, q, qmax, Pf? maybe not
```
If we normalize p sum=1, s=1. Use `np.sum(p)` maybe 1. To save, assume 1.

For many score evals, doing `np.fft.rfft` each time. Candidate p generated normalized. Good.

`make_weights(q, qmax, focus)`:

```
if qmax <=0: return np.full(len(q),1/len(q))
z = (q - qmax) / qmax
# clip z >= -50/focus to avoid underflow? np.exp handles underflow.
w = np.exp(focus * z)
wsum = w.sum()
if not finite or wsum<=0: w=np.zeros; w[np.argmax(q)]=1
else: w /= wsum
return w
```

`grad_dense(w, p, N=None)`:
```
n=p.size; N if none next_pow2(4*n)
L=2n-1
neg = np.zeros(N); neg[0]=p[0]
if n>1: neg[N-n+1:N] = p[1:][::-1]
we = np.zeros(N); we[:L]=w
D = 2*np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(neg) * np.fft.rfft(we), N)[:n]
D = np.maximum(D,0)
# enforce sum? Not necessary.
return D
```
Check formula with p[1:][::-1]. For n=5 N large: neg positions N-1=p1,N-2=p2,N-3=p3,N-4=p4. p[1:][::-1]=p4,p3,p2,p1 assigned to N-4..N-1 -> N-4=p4 (j=4 => N-4 yes), N-3=p3, N-2=p2, N-1=p1. Good.

Let's design local_search step in more detail:

```
def local_search(p0, time_limit, rng, stop_if_below=None):
    p = normalize(p0)
    n=p.size
    N=next_pow2(4*n)
    score,q,qmax = score_q(p,N)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
    current_score=score
    stagn=0
    it=0
    focus_cycle=[5,15,40,120,350]
    fi=0
    method_cycle=['mirror','proj','transfer'] # maybe
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it+=1
       # official or stop if below target?
       if stop_if_below and current_score < stop_if_below:
           # still can continue? Could break to save time.
           break
       # update global best? We'll return
       focus=focus_cycle[fi % len]
       if it % 50 ==0: # vary focus
           fi += 1
       w=make_weights(q,qmax,focus)
       D=grad_dense(w,p,N)
       # if D not finite: perturb
       dmean = np.mean(np.abs(D)) + 1e-30
       dspan = D.max() - D.min() + 1e-30
       best_cand_score = current_score
       best_cand = None
       # mirror steps: maybe one with adaptive mult
       for mult in [0.5,2.0,8.0]:
           cand=mirror_update(p,D,mult/dmean)
           sc, cq, cqmax = score_q(cand,N)
           if sc < best_cand_score:
               best_cand_score=sc; best_cand=cand
       # proj steps
       for mult in [0.5,3.0,15.0]:
           z=p - (mult/(n*dmean))*D
           cand=proj_simplex(z)
           sc,cq,cqmax=score_q(cand,N)
           if sc < best_cand_score: ...
       # transfer
       for frac in [0.05,0.2]:
           cand=transfer_update(p,D,frac)
           sc,cq,cqmax=score_q(cand,N)
           if ...
       if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < current_score - 1e-12:
           p=best_cand; current_score=best_cand_score; q=best_q? Need store q for next. We have cq from best; store.
           if current_score < best_score - 1e-12: best_p=p.copy(); best_score=current_score
           stagn=0
       else:
           stagn += 1
           # if stagnant, try a stronger random restart from best
           if stagn % 30 == 0:
               strength = min(5.0, 0.2*stagn)
               p_new = random_perturb(best_p, rng, strength)
               # maybe apply one mirror step with D to p_new? But D from old p. Use threshold.
               sc_new,q_new,qmax_new = score_q(p_new,N)
               # Accept if better than best or just set current even if worse? For escape, set current=p_new, current_score=sc_new
               p=p_new; current_score=sc_new; q=q_new; qmax=qmax_new
               stagn=0
               fi = (fi+1) % len
   return best_p, best_score
```

```
def make_basis(n, rng):
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    phi=[]
    phi.append(np.full(n,1.0))
    phi.append(x-0.5)
    phi.append((x-0.5)**2)
    phi.append(np.abs(x-0.5))
    for k in [1,2,3,4]:
        phi.append(np.cos(np.pi*k*x))
        phi.append(np.sin(np.pi*k*x))
    for k in [1,2,3]:
        phi.append(np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x))
        phi.append(np.sin(2*np.pi*k*x))
    # random features freq 1..20
    D_rand=16
    for d in range(D_rand):
        freq = float(rng.integers(1, 21))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
        phi.append(np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x + phase))
        # maybe sin too? That's 32 random; total D ~34
    phi=np.array(phi,dtype=np.float64)
    # standardize columns? Since softmax, scaling affects gradient; standardize to std 1.
    std=phi.std(axis=1,keepdims=True); std[std<1e-12]=1
    phi=phi/std
    return phi
```

Implement loop carefully:
```
def grad_active(weights, p):
    n=p.size; D=np.zeros(n)
    nz = weights > 1e-12
    if nz.sum()==0: return D
    idx = np.flatnonzero(nz)
    wv = weights[idx]
    for k,wk in zip(idx,wv):
        if wk<=0: continue
        s = max(0, k - (n-1))
        e = min(n-1, k)
        if s>e: continue
        pidx_start = k - s
        pidx_end = k - e
        # segment from pidx_end to pidx_start inclusive reversed
        seg = p[pidx_end:pidx_start+1][::-1]
        D[s:e+1] += wk * seg
    D *= 2.0
    return D
```
If p has zeros, fine.

For dense FFT, use N = next_pow2(4*n). Need global? We'll compute in local_search and pass.

Now, parametric optimize. We might not need if high-dim local is enough. But include for global. It will use `compute_gradient` dense/active. In parametric, p stays positive, D maybe. Gradient wrt c. Need compute `phi @ p` many times. Could precompute? p changes. D^T p? Let's write efficient:
```
def param_step(c, phi, n, N, focus, target_q):
    logits = phi.dot(c)
    p = stable_softmax(logits)
    score,q,qmax = score_q(p,N)
    w = compute_weights(q,qmax,focus,target_q)
    D = compute_gradient(w,p,N)
    pd = p*D
    s = float(pd.sum())
    grad = phi.dot(pd) - s * phi.dot(p)
    return p,score,q,qmax,w,grad
```
`phi.dot(p)` each step O(Dn); D~30,n6733 =>200k, fine.

Normalize parent:
```
def normalize_sequence(x):
   arr=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   arr=np.clip(arr,0,None)
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
   s=arr.sum()
   if s<=1e-12: return None
   return arr/s
```

```
def deterministic_initials(n, rng, parent=None):
   pool=[]
   uniform = np.full(n,1/n); pool.append(uniform)
   if parent is not None and len(parent)==n: pool.append(normalize(parent))
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   # edge high (cos pi)
   for a in [0.3,0.6,0.9]: p=1+a*np.cos(np.pi*x); p=np.maximum(p,1e-8); pool.append(normalize(p))
   # two-edge high cos 2pi
   for a in [0.3,0.6]: p=1+a*np.cos(2*np.pi*x); pool.append(normalize(p))
   # asymmetric ramp
   for a in [0.3,0.6]: p=1+a*(2*x-1); pool.append(normalize(p))
   # random
   for sd in [0.2,0.8,2.0]: p=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sd,n)); pool.append(normalize(p))
   return pool
```

```
def compute_weights(q, qmax, focus, target_q=None):
    L=q.size
    if qmax <= 0: return np.full(L,1.0/L)
    z=(q-qmax)/qmax
    soft=np.exp(focus*z)
    ss=soft.sum()
    if ss<=0 or not finite: soft=np.zeros(L); soft[np.argmax(q)]=1
    else: soft/=ss
    if target_q is None or target_q <=0:
        return soft
    hinge = np.maximum(q - target_q, 0.0)
    hs = hinge.sum()
    if hs <= 0: return soft
    hinge /= hs
    # mix: if target_q < qmax*0.95, more hinge? use 0.5
    w = 0.5*soft + 0.5*hinge
    wsum = w.sum()
    if wsum <=0: return soft
    return w/wsum
```

This is like multiplicative weights for LP:

Dual of LP for g: constraints C g ≤ M. Let λ≥0. Primal variable g≥0, maximizing sum. KKT: if g_i>0, ∑_k λ_k C_{k,i}=1; if constraint k tight, λ_k>0. Multiplicative weights:
```

   Cg = convolve(f,g) (with f current p)

```

`_stable_softmax(z)`:
```
z = np.asarray(z)

v = np.exp(z)
s = v.sum()
if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
    return np.full(z.shape, 1.0/z.size)
return v/s
```
For 1D.

`_normalize(p)`:
```
p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
p = np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p>0), p, 0.0)
s = p.sum()
if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
    return np.full(p.size, 1.0/p.size) if p.size else None
return p/s
```
If p.size=0, return empty? Not used.

`_score_q(p, N)`:
```
n=p.size
if N is None: N=_fft_len(n)
Pf=np.fft.rfft(p, N)
q=np.fft.irfft(Pf*Pf, N)[:2*n-1]
q=np.maximum(q,0.0)
qm=float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
if not np.isfinite(qm): qm=0.0
s=float(np.sum(p))
score = 2.0*n*qm/(s*s) if s>0 else np.inf
if not np.isfinite(score): score=np.inf
return score,q,qm
```

`_grad_active(weights,p,threshold=1e-12)`:
```
n=p.size; D=np.zeros(n)
nz = weights > threshold
cnt=nz.sum()
if cnt==0: return D
idx=np.flatnonzero(nz)
wvals=weights[idx]
# sort? no
for k,wk in zip(idx,wvals):
   s = k - (n-1)
   if s < 0: s = 0
   e = k
   if e >= n: e = n-1
   if s>e: continue
   p_start = k - s
   p_end = k - e
   # p_end <= p_start
   seg = p[p_end:p_start+1][::-1]
   D[s:e+1] += wk*seg
D *= 2.0
D[~np.isfinite(D)] = 0
return D
```
Check p_end:p_start+1 length. Example k=0,n=5: s=0,e=0,p_start=0,p_end=0 -> seg p[0:1][::-1]=[p0]. D[0]+=2wk p0. Derivative q0 wrt p0=2p0, correct. k=6,n=5: s=4,e=4,p_start=2,p_end=2 -> p2? Wait q6 pairs i+j=6: only i=2,j=4? n=5 indices 0..4 max sum8; k=6 has pairs (2,4),(3,3),(4,2): derivative wrt p4 should 2 p2? Formula for a=4, k=6: 2p_{2}. Good.

`_grad_fft(weights,p,N)`:
```
n=p.size; L=2*n-1
neg=np.zeros(N)
neg[0]=p[0]
if n>1: neg[N-n+1:N] = p[1:][::-1]
we=np.zeros(N)
we[:L]=weights
D=2.0*np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(neg)*np.fft.rfft(we), N)[:n]
D=np.maximum(D,0.0)
D[~np.isfinite(D)] = 0
return D
```
We need avoid `p[1:][::-1]` issue for n=1; handled.

`_mirror_update(p,D,eta)`:

```
u -= u.max()

```
If eta*D negative? D nonnegative; no.

`_proj_simplex(z)`:
```
n=z.size
if n==0: return z
if not finite: z = np.where(finite,z,0)
z0 = z - z.max()
# if all close? Use bisection
lo = z.min(); hi = z.max()
if hi - lo < 1e-15: return np.full(n,1/n)
for _ in range(50):
   mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
   s=np.sum(np.maximum(z-mid,0))
   if s > 1: lo=mid
   else: hi=mid
theta=(lo+hi)*0.5
return np.maximum(z-theta,0)
```
Since sum at theta=z.min() could be <=1 if z all equal? handled. Bisection 50 maybe enough.

`_transfer_update(p,D,frac)`:
```
n=p.size; m=max(1,int(n*0.2))
order=np.argsort(D)
low=order[:m]; high=order[-m:]
mass=frac*p[high].sum()
if mass <= 1e-300: return p.copy()
newp=p.copy()
newp[high] *= (1.0 - frac)
weights = np.maximum(D.max() - D[low], 1e-15)
sw=weights.sum()
if sw<=0:
    weights = np.ones(n); # but only low
else: weights/=sw
newp[low] += mass*weights
return _normalize(newp)
```
If frac=0.5, high multiplied 0.5; mass half sum high. Fine.

`_random_perturb(p, rng, strength)`:


`_parametric_optimize(n, time_slice, rng, min_target_q)`:
- If n<16 or time_slice<20: return uniform maybe.
- Build basis. Need deterministic with rng. To avoid too much randomness, use seed.
- Precompute basis as 2D array (D,n). D maybe 30. `phi.T?` We'll store shape (D,n). `phi.dot(c)` yields length n.
- `c = rng.normal(0,1.0,D)`? Starting zero gives uniform. Start zero for stability.
- `p = _stable_softmax(phi.dot(c))`; score.
- `best_p=p; best_score=score`
- `step=1.0`; `stagn=0`; `focus_idx=0`; focus_cycles=[10,30,100].
- Loop while time:
   - compute D using weights with min_target_q.
   - `pd = p*D`; `s=pd.sum()`; `grad = phi.dot(pd) - s*phi.dot(p)`; norm.
   - if norm small: break or random.
   - direction = -grad/norm.
   - Try lengths [step, 0.5*step, 2*step]: c_try = c + length*direction. p_try = softmax; score_try. Choose best if < best_score? For local descent, if < current score, accept. We want global best; accept improvement over current best? If a step worsens but leads to escape, not track? We'll only accept if improves best_score or current_score. For convergence, accept if improves current_score even if not best? It will improve best if current was best. We'll set if sc < best_score update; if sc < current_sc accept. Also track best.
   - If accepted: c=c_best; p=p_best; current=sc; step=min(step*1.3, 20); stagn=0
   - else: step=max(step*0.7, 0.05); stagn++; if stagn>80: c=rng.normal(0,2,D); p=softmax; sc=score; if sc<best?; current=sc; step=1; stagn=0
   - focus_idx=(focus_idx+1)%3 every 20.
- Return best_p,best_score.
Line search candidates: 3 score evals. Plus D one gradient. Could be heavy. Maybe limit iterations by time. If time_slice 300s, maybe 1000 iter. Good.

But `phi.dot(p)` and `phi.dot(pd)` O(30*6733) ~200k per iter, okay.

Need ensure `c` not grow huge causing underflow. If norm direction, step <=20. c can drift; okay. Softmax stable.

Parametric target_q: min_target_q = target_score/(2*n). If target_score inf, set 1.45/(2n).

`_local_search(p0, time_slice, rng, min_target_q)`:
- Use p0 normalized. If p0 is None uniform.
- `N=_fft_len(n)`.
- score,q,qmax = _score_q(p,N)
- best_p,p_best_score.

```
def _normalize(p):
    arr=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=arr.size
    if n==0: return None
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr > 0.0), arr, 0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
       return np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    return arr/s
```
If all zeros, uniform. Good.

`_deterministic_initials` code:
```
def _deterministic_initials(n, rng, parent=None):
    out=[]
    u=np.full(n,1.0/n); out.append(u)
    if parent is not None:
       np_= _normalize(parent)
       if np_ is not None and len(parent)==n: out.append(np_)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    y=x-0.5
    for a in (0.3,0.6,0.9):
       p=1.0 + a*np.cos(np.pi*x) # at right 0.1..; symmetric? cos πx not symmetric? cos(π(1-x))=-cosπx, not. This is asymmetric edge high left low right. Include.
       p=np.maximum(p,1e-8); out.append(_normalize(p))
       p2=1.0 + a*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x); out.append(_normalize(p2))
    p=1.0 + 0.5*(2.0*x-1.0); out.append(_normalize(p))
    p=1.0 - 0.5*(2.0*x-1.0); out.append(_normalize(p))
    p=np.exp(-((x)/max(0.02,0.08))**2) + np.exp(-((x-1.0)/max(0.02,0.08))**2) + 1e-8; out.append(_normalize(p))
    for sd in (0.3,1.0):
       p=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sd,n)); out.append(_normalize(p))
    # symmetrize some
    for p in list(out[:]):
       ps=0.5*(p+p[::-1]); out.append(_normalize(ps))
    # unique maybe not necessary
    return out
```
This may create many duplicates; okay. But local on top 3 only.

```
def _make_param_basis(n,rng):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    y=x-0.5
    cols=[]
    cols.append(y*y)
    cols.append(y**4)
    cols.append(np.abs(y))
    for k in range(1,7): cols.append(np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x))
    for idx in range(20):
       f=int(rng.integers(1, 21))
       sign=1.0 if rng.random()<0.5 else -1.0
       cols.append(sign*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*x))
    phi=np.array(cols,float)
    # standardize
    std=phi.std(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    std[np.isclose(std,0)] = 1.0
    phi = phi / std
    return phi
```
D=26. If n small, frequencies high alias, okay.

```python
"""
Searches for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))**2.

Strategy:
- Normalize sequences to sum 1 (scale invariant objective).
- Use fast FFT convolution and a smooth log-sum-exp/peak penalty.
- Run low-dimensional symmetric parameterized global search, then dense
  local search with mirror / projected-gradient / mass-transfer moves.
- Periodically verify with the authoritative evaluate_sequence().
"""

import numpy as np
import time


BASE_TARGET_SCORE = 1.42


def _uniform(n):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)


def _normalize(p):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr > 0.0), arr, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return _uniform(n)
    return arr / s


def _clean_seq(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        p = _uniform(1024)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p >= 0.0), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        p = _uniform(p.size)
    else:
        p = p / s
    return [float(x) for x in p]


def _fft_len(n):
    # Enough length for linear convolution, and for the negative-index
    # helper used in gradient computation.
    x = max(16, 4 * int(n) + 8)
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def _score_q(p, N):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    if N is None or N < 2 * n - 1:
        N = _fft_len(n)

    Pf = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(Pf * Pf, N)
    q = q[:2 * n - 1]
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)

    qmax = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(qmax) or qmax < 0.0:
        qmax = 0.0

    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return np.inf, q, qmax

    score = 2.0 * float(n) * qmax / (s * s)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = np.inf
    return score, q, qmax


def _stable_softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -1e300)
    if z.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    v = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return _uniform(z.size)
    return v / s


def _compute_weights(q, qmax, focus, min_target_q):
    L = q.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if qmax <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)

    z = (q - qmax) / qmax
    soft = np.exp(float(focus) * z)
    ss = float(np.sum(soft))
    if not np.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 0.0:
        soft = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        soft[int(np.argmax(q))] = 1.0
    else:
        soft = soft / ss

    if min_target_q is None or not np.isfinite(min_target_q) or min_target_q <= 0.0:
        target = 0.9 * qmax
    else:
        # Attack peaks that are at least 10% below current max once feasible.
        target = max(float(min_target_q), 0.9 * qmax)

    hinge = q - target
    hinge[hinge < 0.0] = 0.0
    hs = float(np.sum(hinge))
    if hs <= 1e-300:
        return soft
    hinge = hinge / hs

    w = 0.5 * soft + 0.5 * hinge
    ws = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        return soft
    return w / ws


def _grad_active(weights, p):
    n = p.size
    D = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.flatnonzero(weights > 1e-12)
    if idx.size == 0:
        return D

    for kk in idx:
        k = int(kk)
        wk = float(weights[k])
        if wk <= 0.0:
            continue
        s = k - (n - 1)
        if s < 0:
            s = 0
        e = k
        if e >= n:
            e = n - 1
        if s > e:
            continue
        p_start = k - s
        p_end = k - e
        # p[k-i] for i=s..e, i increasing => p index decreasing.
        seg = p[p_end:p_start + 1][::-1]
        D[s:e + 1] += wk * seg
    D *= 2.0
    D[~np.isfinite(D)] = 0.0
    D[D < 0.0] = 0.0
    return D


def _grad_fft(weights, p, N):
    n = p.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if N is None or N < 4 * n:
        N = _fft_len(n)

    neg = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 0:
        neg[0] = p[0]
    if n > 1:
        neg[N - n + 1:N] = p[1:][::-1]

    we = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    we[:L] = weights

    D = 2.0 * np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(neg) * np.fft.rfft(we), N)[:n]
    D = np.maximum(D, 0.0)
    D[~np.isfinite(D)] = 0.0
    return D


def _compute_gradient(weights, p, N):
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    nz = int(np.count_nonzero(weights > 1e-12))
    if nz <= 192:
        return _grad_active(weights, p)
    return _grad_fft(weights, p, N)


def _mirror_update(p, D, eta):
    n = p.size
    floor = 1e-16 / float(n)
    ps = np.maximum(p, floor)
    u = np.log(ps) - float(eta) * D
    u = np.where(np.isfinite(u), u, -700.0)
    u -= float(np.max(u))
    u = np.clip(u, -700.0, 0.0)
    v = np.exp(u)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return _uniform(n)
    return v / s


def _proj_simplex(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    n = z.size
    if n == 0:
        return z
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
    lo = float(np.min(z))
    hi = float(np.max(z))
    if hi - lo < 1e-15:
        return _uniform(n)

    for _ in range(60):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = float(np.sum(np.maximum(z - mid, 0.0)))
        if s > 1.0:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    theta = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    v = np.maximum(z - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return _uniform(n)
    return v / s


def _transfer_update(p, D, frac):
    p = _normalize(p)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    D = np.asarray(D, dtype=np.float64)
    if D.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(D)):
        return p
    if frac <= 0.0:
        return p.copy()
    frac = min(float(frac), 0.9)

    m = max(1, int(0.2 * n))
    order = np.argsort(D)
    low = order[:m]
    high = order[-m:]

    mass = frac * float(np.sum(p[high]))
    if mass <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()

    newp = p.copy()
    newp[high] *= (1.0 - frac)

    w = np.maximum(float(np.max(D)) - D[low], 1e-15)
    ws = float(np.sum(w))
    if ws <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w = w / ws

    newp[low] += mass * w
    return _normalize(newp)


def _random_perturb(p, rng, strength):
    p = _normalize(p)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    if strength <= 0.0:
        return p.copy()
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(strength), n)
    noise = np.clip(noise, -30.0, 30.0)
    p2 = p * np.exp(noise)
    return _normalize(p2)


def _deterministic_initials(n, rng, parent=None):
    out = []
    u = _uniform(n)
    out.append(u)

    if parent is not None:
        par = _normalize(parent)
        if par is not None and par.size == n:
            out.append(par)

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    y = x - 0.5

    for a in (0.3, 0.6, 0.9):
        p = np.maximum(1.0 + a * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x), 1e-9)
        out.append(_normalize(p))
        p = np.maximum(1.0 + a * np.cos(np.pi * x), 1e-9)
        out.append(_normalize(p))

    out.append(_normalize(np.maximum(1.0 + 0.7 * (2.0 * x - 1.0), 1e-9)))
    out.append(_normalize(np.maximum(1.0 - 0.7 * (2.0 * x - 1.0), 1e-9)))

    sig = max(0.03, 0.08)
    p = np.exp(-((x / sig) ** 2)) + np.exp(-(((x - 1.0) / sig) ** 2)) + 1e-9
    out.append(_normalize(p))

    for sd in (0.3, 1.0, 2.5):
        p = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sd, n))
        out.append(_normalize(p))

    # Symmetrize a few to help exploit the reversal symmetry.
    for p in list(out[:8]):
        ps = 0.5 * (p + p[::-1])
        out.append(_normalize(ps))

    return out


def _make_param_basis(n, rng):
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    y = x - 0.5
    cols = []
    cols.append(y * y)
    cols.append(y ** 4)
    cols.append(np.abs(y))
    for k in range(1, 7):
        cols.append(np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(k) * x))
    for _ in range(20):
        f = int(rng.integers(1, 21))
        sign = 1.0 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1.0
        cols.append(sign * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * x))

    phi = np.array(cols, dtype=np.float64)
    std = phi.std(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    std[~np.isfinite(std)] = 1.0
    std[std < 1e-12] = 1.0
    phi = phi / std
    return phi


def _parametric_optimize(n, time_slice, rng, min_target_q):
    p = _uniform(n)
    if time_slice <= 5.0 or n < 16:
        score, _, _ = _score_q(p, None)
        return p, score

    N = _fft_len(n)
    phi = _make_param_basis(n, rng)
    Ddim = phi.shape[0]

    c = np.zeros(Ddim, dtype=np.float64)
    p = _stable_softmax(phi.dot(c))
    score, q, qmax = _score_q(p, N)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = score
    current_score = score
    step = 1.0
    stagn = 0
    focus_cycle = (10.0, 30.0, 100.0, 300.0)
    fi = 0

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(time_slice) - 2.0)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        focus = focus_cycle[fi % len(focus_cycle)]
        w = _compute_weights(q, qmax, focus, min_target_q)
        G = _compute_gradient(w, p, N)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(G)):
            break

        pd = p * G
        s = float(np.sum(pd))
        grad = phi.dot(pd) - s * (phi.dot(p))
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm <= 1e-15:
            break

        direction = -grad / gnorm
        best_try = None
        best_try_score = current_score
        for length in (step, 0.5 * step, 2.0 * step):
            c_try = c + length * direction
            p_try = _stable_softmax(phi.dot(c_try))
            sc_try, _, _ = _score_q(p_try, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_try_score - 1e-12:
                best_try_score = sc_try
                best_try = (c_try, p_try)

        if best_try is not None:
            c, p = best_try
            current_score = best_try_score
            if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = current_score
                best_p = p.copy()
            step = min(20.0, step * 1.4)
            stagn = 0
        else:
            step = max(0.05, step * 0.65)
            stagn += 1
            fi += 1
            if stagn > 80:
                c = rng.normal(0.0, 1.5, Ddim)
                p = _stable_softmax(phi.dot(c))
                current_score, q, qmax = _score_q(p, N)
                if np.isfinite(current_score) and current_score < best_score:
                    best_score = current_score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                step = 1.0
                stagn = 0

        # Refresh q/qmax for next gradient when accepted.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
            _, q, qmax = _score_q(p, N)

    return best_p, best_score


def _local_search(p0, time_slice, rng, min_target_q):
    p = _normalize(p0)
    if p is None:
        p = _uniform(1024)
    n = p.size

    N = _fft_len(n)
    current_score, q, qmax = _score_q(p, N)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = current_score
    current_score_b = current_score

    focus_cycle = (10.0, 30.0, 100.0, 300.0)
    fi = 0
    stagn = 0
    scale = 1.0

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(time_slice) - 2.0)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        focus = focus_cycle[(fi + (stagn // 60)) % len(focus_cycle)]
        w = _compute_weights(q, qmax, focus, min_target_q)
        D = _compute_gradient(w, p, N)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(D)):
            break

        dmean = float(np.mean(np.abs(D))) + 1e-30

        best_cand = None
        best_cand_q = None
        best_cand_qmax = 0.0
        best_cand_score = current_score

        # Mirror / exponentiated gradient moves.
        for mult in (1.0 * scale, 4.0 * scale):
            cand = _mirror_update(p, D, mult / dmean)
            sc, cq, cqmax = _score_q(cand, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score - 1e-12:
                best_cand = cand
                best_cand_q = cq
                best_cand_qmax = cqmax
                best_cand_score = sc

        # Projected gradient moves.
        for mult in (10.0 * scale, 50.0 * scale):
            z = p - (mult / (float(n) * dmean)) * D
            cand = _proj_simplex(z)
            sc, cq, cqmax = _score_q(cand, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score - 1e-12:
                best_cand = cand
                best_cand_q = cq
                best_cand_qmax = cqmax
                best_cand_score = sc

        # Explicit mass transfer from high-gradient to low-gradient coordinates.
        for frac in (0.1 * min(1.0, scale), 0.3 * min(1.0, scale)):
            cand = _transfer_update(p, D, frac)
            sc, cq, cqmax = _score_q(cand, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score - 1e-12:
                best_cand = cand
                best_cand_q = cq
                best_cand_qmax = cqmax
                best_cand_score = sc

        accepted = False
        if best_cand is not None:
            if best_cand_score < current_score - 1e-12:
                p = best_cand
                q = best_cand_q
                qmax = best_cand_qmax
                current_score = best_cand_score
                accepted = True
            elif best_cand_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                p = best_cand
                q = best_cand_q
                qmax = best_cand_qmax
                current_score = best_cand_score
                accepted = True

            if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = current_score
                best_p = p.copy()

        if accepted:
            stagn = 0
            scale = min(6.0, scale * 1.1)
        else:
            stagn += 1
            scale = min(12.0, scale * 1.05)
            fi += 1

            if stagn >= 40:
                strength = min(3.0, 0.04 * float(stagn))
                p = _random_perturb(best_p, rng, strength)
                # Occasionally enforce reversal symmetry; it is an exact symmetry
                # of the objective and often helps escape asymmetric traps.
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    p = 0.5 * (p + p[::-1])
                p = _normalize(p)
                current_score, q, qmax = _score_q(p, N)
                stagn = 0
                scale = 1.0
                fi = (fi + 1) % len(focus_cycle)

            if stagn > 350:
                p = _random_perturb(best_p, rng, 4.0)
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    p = 0.5 * (p + p[::-1])
                p = _normalize(p)
                current_score, q, qmax = _score_q(p, N)
                stagn = 0
                scale = 1.0

    return best_p, best_score


def _safe_official(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)  # expected to be provided by environment
    except NameError:
        return np.inf
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    return val


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Returns the best sequence found before the time budget expires."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)
    budget = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 2400.0
    if budget <= 5.0:
        try:
            par = _normalize(height_sequence_1)  # noqa: F821
        except Exception:
            par = None
        if par is not None:
            return _clean_seq(par)
        return _clean_seq(_uniform(1024))

    # Parent sequence, if available.
    parent_arr = None
    try:
        parent_raw = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
        parent_arr = _normalize(parent_raw)
    except Exception:
        parent_arr = None

    n_parent = parent_arr.size if parent_arr is not None else 0

    # Baseline.
    if parent_arr is not None:
        baseline_arr = parent_arr
    else:
        baseline_arr = _uniform(6733)

    baseline_seq = _clean_seq(baseline_arr)
    best_official_score = _safe_official(baseline_seq)
    best_official_seq = None
    if np.isfinite(best_official_score):
        best_official_seq = baseline_seq

    # Internal best (used if official evaluator is unavailable or fails).
    best_internal_score, _, _ = _score_q(baseline_arr, None)
    if not np.isfinite(best_internal_score):
        best_internal_score = 2.0
    best_internal_seq = baseline_arr.copy()

    # Candidate lengths.
    base_lengths = [1024, 2048, 4096, 6733]
    if 64 <= n_parent <= 12000:
        base_lengths.append(int(n_parent))

    length_set = set()
    for nn in base_lengths:
        if 64 <= nn <= 12000:
            length_set.add(int(nn))
    lengths = sorted(length_set)

    if len(lengths) > 4:
        keep = []
        if n_parent:
            keep.append(int(n_parent))
        for nn in sorted(lengths, reverse=True):
            if nn not in keep:
                keep.append(nn)
            if len(keep) == 4:
                break
        lengths = keep
    else:
        lengths = sorted(lengths)

    # Order: parent first if present, then larger lengths.
    order = []
    if n_parent:
        order.append(int(n_parent))
    for nn in sorted(lengths, reverse=True):
        if nn not in order:
            order.append(nn)

    max_n = max(order) if order else 6733
    weights = []
    for nn in order:
        ww = 0.25 + 0.75 * (float(nn) / float(max_n))
        if n_parent and nn == int(n_parent):
            ww += 1.5
        weights.append(ww)
    total_w = float(sum(weights))
    total_s = max(30.0, budget - 20.0)
    slices = [total_s * ww / total_w for ww in weights]

    global_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(5.0, budget - 12.0)
    candidate_list = []

    for nn, slice_s in zip(order, slices):
        now = time.monotonic()
        remaining_global = global_deadline - now
        if remaining_global <= 30.0:
            break
        slice_s = min(float(slice_s), remaining_global - 10.0)
        if slice_s <= 5.0:
            continue

        parent_this = None
        if n_parent and int(n_parent) == int(nn) and parent_arr is not None:
            parent_this = parent_arr

        if np.isfinite(best_official_score):
            target_score = max(1.0, min(BASE_TARGET_SCORE, best_official_score * 0.98))
        else:
            target_score = BASE_TARGET_SCORE

        target_q = target_score / (2.0 * float(nn))

        pool = _deterministic_initials(nn, rng, parent_this)

        # Low-dimensional global search, when there is enough time.
        if slice_s > 90.0:
            pt = min(0.32 * slice_s, slice_s - 20.0)
            if pt > 20.0:
                tstart = time.monotonic()
                p_par, sc_par = _parametric_optimize(nn, pt - 1.0, rng, target_q)
                used = time.monotonic() - tstart
                if p_par is not None:
                    pool.append(p_par)
                if np.isfinite(sc_par) and sc_par < best_internal_score:
                    best_internal_score = sc_par
                    best_internal_seq = p_par.copy()
                # Do not explicitly track used; local search deadline below
                # will use the same global deadline.

        N = _fft_len(nn)

        # Rank initial candidates by fast internal score.
        evals = []
        for i, pp in enumerate(pool):
            pp = _normalize(pp)
            if pp is None:
                continue
            sc, _, _ = _score_q(pp, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                evals.append((sc, i, pp))
        evals.sort()

        local_deadline = min(global_deadline - 8.0, now + slice_s - 5.0)
        top = evals[:3]
        fracs = (0.55, 0.25, 0.20)

        length_best_p = None
        length_best_sc = np.inf
        length_best_seq = None

        for idx, (sc0, _, p0) in enumerate(top):
            if time.monotonic() >= local_deadline - 2.0:
                break
            alloc = slice_s * fracs[idx]
            tt = max(5.0, min(float(time.monotonic() - (local_deadline - slice_s)), alloc))
            tt = min(tt, local_deadline - time.monotonic() - 2.0)
            if tt <= 1.0:
                continue

            p_best, sc_best = _local_search(p0, tt, rng, target_q)
            if p_best is None:
                continue
            if np.isfinite(sc_best) and sc_best < length_best_sc:
                length_best_sc = sc_best
                length_best_p = p_best.copy()
                length_best_seq = _clean_seq(p_best)

            if np.isfinite(sc_best) and sc_best < best_internal_score:
                best_internal_score = sc_best
                best_internal_seq = p_best.copy()

            if length_best_seq is not None:
                candidate_list.append((nn, length_best_seq, length_best_sc))

        if length_best_seq is not None:
            off = _safe_official(length_best_seq)
            if np.isfinite(off) and (not np.isfinite(best_official_score) or off < best_official_score):
                best_official_score = off
                best_official_seq = length_best_seq
            candidate_list.append((nn, length_best_seq, length_best_sc))

    # Final verification of retained candidates.
    for nn, seq, _ in candidate_list:
        if not seq:
            continue
        off = _safe_official(seq)
        if np.isfinite(off) and (not np.isfinite(best_official_score) or off < best_official_score):
            best_official_score = off
            best_official_seq = seq

    # Compare against baseline once more.
    off = _safe_official(baseline_seq)
    if np.isfinite(off) and (not np.isfinite(best_official_score) or off < best_official_score):
        best_official_score = off
        best_official_seq = baseline_seq

    if best_official_seq is not None:
        return best_official_seq

    # Fallback: best internally scored sequence.
    return _clean_seq(best_internal_seq)
```
